===== Page 12 =====

Expression Tree
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
implementation: stack should have one element -  root 
of the expression tree.
•
Pass this as argument to eval_recursive.
•
If leaf, then its an operand – find and return the 
numeric value of the character.
•
Evaluate left then evaluate right
•
Based on the operator, combine the two results.
int eval_recursive(node_t* root)
{
    int left, right;
    if (root->left_ == NULL && 
root->right_ == NULL)
        return root->ch_ - '0';
 
    left = eval_recursive(root->left_);
    right = eval_recursive(root->right_);
 
 
 
switch (root->ch_)
    {
        case '+': return left + right;
        case '-': return left - right;
        case '*': return left * right;
        case '/': return left / right;
    }
     return 0;
}
 int eval(stack_t *ptr_stack)
{
return eval_recursive(pop(ptr_stack));
}

Heap
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026

B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
A heap(maxheap) is a tree with two special properties.
1. Complete binary tree:
The nodes are filled up at each level.
At the bottom most level, leftmost nodes are filled up.
2.     Parental dominance:
The key at the root is greater than the keys of its subtrees 
recursively.

----------------------------------------

Three 8-Node Binary Trees
1. MAX HEAP 2. NOT A HEAP 3. NOT A HEAP
(parental dominance + complete) (parental dominance violated) (incomplete binary tree)
e e e missing
1 0
v Complete binary tree v Complete binary tree X NOT complete: position 6 is empty
8 nodes 8 nodes 8 nodes

Heap

Heap Implementation:
•
Always as an array
•
Method 1:
•Start from index 0
•
Leftchild(i) = 2i + 1 
•
Rightchild(i) = 2i + 2 
•
Parent(i) = floor((i-1)/2)
•
Method 2:
•
Start from index 1
•
Leftchild(i) = 2i 
•
Rightchild(i) = 2i + 1
•
Parent(i) = floor(i/2)
•
We will use the method 2

Advantages of heap:

Conversion of a given array to a heap
•
Heapify:
•
Make the heap of n nodes 
heapify 
about the ith node
static void heapify(int a[], int n, int pos)
{
int i = pos;
int j = 2 * i;
int val = a[i];
int is_heap = 0;
 
while(j <= n && ! is_heap){
if(j + 1 <= n && a[j+1] > a[j])
++j;
if(a[j] > val) {
a[i] = a[j];
i = j;
j = 2 * i;
}
else {
is_heap = 1;
}
 }
a[i] = val;
}

Implement insert a node at the end and remove the maximum
•
Refer the folder heap2 – heap.h heap.c 
•
Insert : add at the end; check whether the parent tree is a heap repeatedly 
until the root is reached
void insert(heap_t *ptr_heap, int key)
{
int pos = ++ptr_heap->n_;
ptr_heap->key_[pos] = key;
while(pos > 1 && ptr_heap->key_[pos] > ptr_heap->key_[pos/2])
{
swap(&ptr_heap->key_[pos], &ptr_heap->key_[pos/2]);
pos = pos / 2;
}
}
•

===== Page 23 =====

Heap
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026

B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
Implement insert a node at the end and remove the maximum
•
Refer the folder heap2 – heap.h heap.c 
•
Delete max : replace the first node with the last and heapify about the root.
int delete_max(heap_t *ptr_heap)
{
int res = ptr_heap->key_[1];
ptr_heap->key_[1] = ptr_heap->key_[ptr_heap->n_];
heapify(ptr_heap->key_, --ptr_heap->n_, 1);
return res;
}

{

struct node* next_;
};
typedef struct node node_t;

{

};
typedef struct queue queue_t;

Implement a priority queue using heap 
void enqueue(queue_t *ptr_queue, int key)
{
insert(&ptr_queue->q_, key);
 
}
int dequeue(queue_t *ptr_queue)
{
return delete_max(&ptr_queue->q_);
}

Implement a priority queue using heap 
•
In other implementations of priority queue, one of the operations(enqueue 
or dequeue) would not be that efficient – the number of operations would 
depend on the number of elements in the queue
•
In this implementation, both the operations are proportional to log(n, 2) 
where is the number of elements

AVL tree
AVL tree: height balanced binary search tree
•
Searching in a binary search tree may require n comparisons
•
The tree could become skewed – degenerate to a list
•
AVL tree gives a solution to this problem
•
Each node has a balance factor
•
Height(left subtree) – Height(right subtree)
•
Difference is maintained within 1 (-1, 0, 1)
•
Rotate the tree to keep the balance
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B. SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12

Right rotation

Left rotation
LR

Left rotation + Right rotation
RL

Right rotation + Left rotation
Same direction → single rotation
Opposite directions → double rotation

AVL tree
AVL tree: LR Rotation

The BST becomes:
        30
       /
      10
        \
         20
The path is:
30 → 10 → 20
The first move is left, but the second is right. Therefore:
Left → Right
This is an LR case. (requires double rotation)

===== Page 2 =====

Splay Tree
Splay Tree:  
•
A Splay Tree is a self-adjusting Binary Search Tree (BST)
•
Whenever a node is accessed, move that node to the root
•
This operation is called splaying
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B. SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12

----------------------------------------

===== Page 3 =====

Splay Tree
Splay Tree: salient features 
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B. SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12

No Extra Metadata: They require no balance accounting data (like color 
bits in red-black trees or height numbers in AVL trees), saving memory. 

Locality of Reference: If we access a piece of data once, it moves to the 
top, making subsequent lookups of the same data instantaneous. 

Efficiency: While a single worst-case operation can take time proportional 
to the number of elements, a sequence of m operations is guaranteed to 
run in time proportional to m * log(n, 2).

Dynamic Adaptation: The tree structure continuously morphs to fit the 
actual access pattern of the workload rather than enforcing a rigid shape.

----------------------------------------

Splay Tree
Splay Tree: ZIG
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B. SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12

Node accessed is the child of the root
Suppose we access 30:
       50
      /
     30
Perform a right rotation:
       30
         \
          50
Suppose we access 70
       50
         \
          70
perform a left rotation:
       70
      /
     50
Zig rule
Node is child of root
        ↓
Perform one rotation
        ↓
Node becomes root

Splay Tree
Splay Tree: ZIG-ZIG (LL case)
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B. SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12

node and its parent are both on the same side
         50
       /
      30
     /
    20
We want to access 20.
Here:
20 is the left child of 30 
30 is the left child of 50 
So this is Left-Left (LL).
First rotation
Rotate 50 right:
        30
       /  \
      20   50
Second rotation
Rotate 30 right:
        20
          \
           30
             \
              50
Zig-ZIG rule
LL => two right rotations

Splay Tree
Splay Tree: ZIG-ZAG  
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B. SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12

node and its parent are both on the opposite side
 Consider:
        50
       /
      20
        \
         30
30 is the right child of 20 
20 is the left child of 50 
Therefore:
Left-Right (LR) configuration.
First rotation
Rotate 20 left:
        50
       /
      30
     /
    20
Second rotation
Rotate 50 right:
        30
       /  \
      20   50
So 30 becomes the root.
Zig-Zag rule
LR => one left; one right

•
Step-by-Step Algorithm for Each Incoming Number x
•
1. Insert:
•
 If left_half is empty or x <= root(left_half), 
•
push x into left_half.
•
 Otherwise, push x into right_half.

•
2. Balance:
•
If the size of left_half exceeds right_half by more than 1
•
  pop the root from left_half and push it into right_half.
•
If the size of right_half exceeds left_half, 
•
  pop the root from right_half and push it into left_half.

Graph
Definition
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
A graph is a non-linear data structure used to represent 
relationships between objects. It consists of vertices 
(nodes) and edges connecting pairs of vertices.
G = (V, E)
V is the set of vertices and E is the set of edges.
There are 8 vertices or nodes. 
V = { A, B, C, D, E, F, G, H}
There are 11 edges. 
AB, AC, BC, BD, CE, DE, DF, FG, EG, GH, FH

----------------------------------------

===== Page 3 =====

Graph
Terminology
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
Vertex (node): An object represented in the graph, such as A.
Edge: A connection between two vertices, such as AB.
Adjacent vertices: Vertices connected directly by an edge; A and B are adjacent.
Incident edge: An edge is incident on each endpoint; AB is incident on A and B.
Degree: The number of edges incident on a vertex in an undirected graph.
In-degree: The number of directed edges entering a vertex.
Out-degree: The number of directed edges leaving a vertex.
Path: A sequence of vertices joined by edges, such as A–B–D–F–H.
Path length: The number of edges in a path; A–B–D–F–H has length 4.

----------------------------------------

===== Page 4 =====

Graph
Terminology
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
Cycle: A path returning to its starting vertex without repeating intermediate 
vertices, such as A–B–C–A.
Connected graph: An undirected graph where every vertex is reachable from 
every other vertex.
Connected component: A maximal connected part of a graph.
Simple graph: A graph without self-loops or multiple edges between the same 
pair of vertices.
Self-loop: An edge connecting a vertex to itself.
Complete graph: A simple undirected graph where every pair of distinct vertices 
is connected.
Subgraph: A graph formed from a subset of another graph's vertices and edges.
Cyclic / acyclic graph: A graph that contains / does not contain a cycle.

example
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026

Number of vertices: |V| = 8.
Number of edges: |E| = 11.
Neighbours of A: B and C.
Degree of A: deg(A) = 2.
Degree of B: deg(B) = 3 (edges AB, BC and BD).
One path from A to H is A–B–D–F–H; its length is 4.
A cycle is A–B–C–A.
The graph is connected because every vertex can be reached from every other 
vertex.

The seven bridges of Konigsberg =

Can you start and end at the same vertex, traversing every

===== Page 7 =====

Graph
Euler graph and Hamilton graph
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
A graph is said to be Eulerian if it supports the traversal of all edges once and only 
once and come back to the starting point. The necessary and sufficient condition 
is that the degree of each node should be even.
A graph is said to be Hamiltonian if it supports the traversal all vertices once and 
only once and come back the starting point. We  do not have an efficient solution 
for this.

----------------------------------------

===== Page 8 =====

Graph
Applications:
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
Road and transport networks: Vertices are cities or intersections; edges are 
roads. Weights can represent distance or travel time.
Social networks: Vertices are people; edges are friendships, follows or 
interactions.
Computer networks: Vertices are computers or routers; edges are 
communication links.
Course prerequisites: A directed edge can mean one course must be completed 
before another.

----------------------------------------

===== Page 9 =====

Graph
Applications:
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
Software dependencies: Vertices are modules, files or tasks; edges represent 
dependencies.
Airline and logistics networks: Vertices are airports or warehouses; edges 
represent routes.
Web pages: Vertices are pages and directed edges represent hyperlinks.
Project scheduling: Vertices represent tasks and edges represent precedence 
constraints

Graph
ADT:
   
© Confidential Information of PES University. All Rights Reserved - 2026 
2 / 9
B.TECH » DEPARTMENT OF COMPUTER SCIENCE & ENGINEERING (AI & ML) » DATA STRUCTURES AND ITS APPLICATIONS » UE25CS252A » UNIT 1 » ARRAYS » CLASS 3/12
Operation
Purpose
create_graph()
Create an empty graph.
add_vertex(v)
Insert a vertex.
remove_vertex(v)
Remove a vertex and its incident edges.
add_edge(u, v)
Add an edge between u and v.
remove_edge(u, v)
Remove an edge.
has_vertex(v)
Check whether v exists.
has_edge(u, v)
Check whether an edge exists.
get_neighbors(v)
Return vertices adjacent to v.
degree(v)
Return the degree of v in an undirected graph.
display_graph()
Display vertices and edges.
destroy_graph()
Release resources used by the graph.

trees
1. Implement tree using an array of nodes. Provide a suitable 
memory manager - which holds indices of all free nodes.
On allocation, it pops an index.
on deallocation, it pushes the index. 
On this tree, support the following functions.
a) count the # of nodes 
b) count the # of leaves 
c) compare two trees 
d) copy a tree to anotherthe 
e) provide different ways of traversal 

also,
f) thread such a tree 
g) make it an expression tree 

2. provide a level order display of a tree    
   (for both the implementations)
   
3. check whether a given tree is a BST (challenging)
4. create a binary tree (not a BST) where the insertion specifies the 
   key of the parent and whether the new node should be to the left or 
   the right of the parent.
